coffee hour anymore, and even when someone tells a funny story I just sit there with a flat face like the feeling can't get out.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where days food just doesn't appeal to me, so I pick at toast or soup and my skirts have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: For most of this whole stretch, the sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been there in the background, even during times when the I can see that. And sometimes it feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things they say. It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are being sent in a way that's unusual or impossible, like through secret codes or telepathy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s telepathy or anything like that. It’s more that I’ll read the church bulletin, or hear something someone says at church or in the walking club, and it feels like it’s about me somehow. Like they’re watching me and slipping in little messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are actually about you, or could they be just coincidences?\n\nPatient: I know they could be coincidences, but to me it really feels like they’re about me. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people watching you and sending messages are part of a larger plan or organization?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s a bigger organization or plan, no. It’s more just certain people at church and in the walking club. I get this feeling they’re watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say, but I can’t really say it’s some larger group behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that people are watching you and sending you messages is something that most people would find strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange or hard to believe. I can see that. But even knowing that, I still can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the messages you think are being sent about you as being unusual or not something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say it’s unusual, yes. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because, even when I’m having a pretty good day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. And sometimes it feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things they say. It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are being sent in a way that's unusual or impossible, like through secret codes or telepathy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s telepathy or anything like that. It’s more that I’ll read the church bulletin, or hear something someone says at church or in the walking club, and it feels like it’s about me somehow. Like they’re watching me and slipping in little messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are actually about you, or could they be just coincidences?\n\nPatient: I know they could be coincidences, but to me it really feels like they’re about me. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people watching you and sending messages are part of a larger plan or organization?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s a bigger organization or plan, no. It’s more just certain people at church and in the walking club. I can see that. But even knowing that, I still can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the messages you think are being sent about you as being unusual or not something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say it’s unusual, yes. But it still feels real to me, like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you're misinterpreting these messages and they're not actually about you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I know there’s a possibility I’m misreading it. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because, even when I’m having a pretty good day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. And sometimes it feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things they say. It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are being sent in a way that's unusual or impossible, like through secret codes or telepathy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s telepathy or anything like that. It’s more that I’ll read the church bulletin, or hear something someone says at church or in the walking club, and it feels like it’s about me somehow. Like they’re watching me and slipping in little messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are actually about you, or could they be just coincidences?\n\nPatient: I know they could be coincidences, but to me it really feels like they’re about me. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people watching you and sending messages are part of a larger plan or organization?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s a bigger organization or plan, no. It’s more just certain people at church and in the walking club. I get this feeling they’re watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say, but I can’t really say it’s some larger group behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that people are watching you and sending you messages is something that most people would find strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange or hard to believe. I can see that. But even knowing that, I still can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the messages you think are being sent about you as being unusual or not something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say it’s unusual, yes. But it still feels real to me, like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you're misinterpreting these messages and they're not actually about you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I know there’s a possibility I’m misreading it. It sounds like my friend’s voice coming from the kitchen or the porch. And sometimes it feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things they say. It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are being sent in a way that's unusual or impossible, like through secret codes or telepathy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s telepathy or anything like that. It’s more that I’ll read the church bulletin, or hear something someone says at church or in the walking club, and it feels like it’s about me somehow. Like they’re watching me and slipping in little messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are actually about you, or could they be just coincidences?\n\nPatient: I know they could be coincidences, but to me it really feels like they’re about me. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people watching you and sending messages are part of a larger plan or organization?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s a bigger organization or plan, no. It’s more just certain people at church and in the walking club. I get this feeling they’re watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say, but I can’t really say it’s some larger group behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that people are watching you and sending you messages is something that most people would find strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange or hard to believe. I can see that. But even knowing that, I still can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the messages you think are being sent about you as being unusual or not something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say it’s unusual, yes. I mean, I know it probably sounds like something that wouldn’t normally happen. But it still feels real to me, like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you're misinterpreting these messages and they're not actually about you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I know there’s a possibility I’m misreading it. I do think about that. But even when I tell myself it might not be about me, I still can’t really shake the feeling that certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages in what they say or in the bulletin.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings that people are watching you and sending you messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been about eleven months now. The voices and the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight… that’s all been going on for around that long.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices earlier, can you tell me more about what you mean by voices?\n\nPatient: Sometimes when the house is quiet, I’ll hear my name called, or just a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or the porch. It’s clear enough that I’ll answer back before I remember nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they telling you to do something or are they more like they're talking about you?\n\nPatient: It’s more just my name, or a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice coming from the kitchen or the porch. I don’t really hear it giving me orders, it’s just clear enough that sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you think they're actually coming from outside your head or are they just your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s coming from outside my head, like from the kitchen or out on the porch. It doesn’t feel like just my own thoughts, because it sounds like my friend’s voice, and sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things - the messages, the voices, and the feeling of being watched - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes... I’ll start trying to explain one thing, like something about the mail or something at church, and then I kind of drift into talking about quilt patterns, or birds, or warnings I noticed. Then I can see the other person looking confused, and I realize I haven’t really said what I meant to say.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and feelings of being watched been constant over the past eleven months, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: They’ve pretty much been going on for about eleven months now. They’re not every single minute, but the voices, the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening through that whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful events or changes in your life around the time these feelings and voices started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes... it started not too long after my close friend died. That was about a year and five months ago. Things seemed to change after that.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the feeling of being watched ever stopped completely, even for a short period, since they started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think they’ve stopped completely. They come and go some during the day, but since it started, the voices and messages have been there off and on the whole eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where these voices and feelings of being watched stopped, and then came back?\n\nPatient: No, not really.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because, even when I’m having a pretty good day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. And sometimes it feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things they say. It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are being sent in a way that's unusual or impossible, like through secret codes or telepathy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s telepathy or anything like that. It’s more that I’ll read the church bulletin, or hear something someone says at church or in the walking club, and it feels like it’s about me somehow. Like they’re watching me and slipping in little messages.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are actually about you, or could they be just coincidences?\n\nPatient: I know they could be coincidences, but to me it really feels like they’re about me. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people watching you and sending messages are part of a larger plan or organization?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s a bigger organization or plan, no. It’s more just certain people at church and in the walking club. I get this feeling they’re watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say, but I can’t really say it’s some larger group behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that people are watching you and sending you messages is something that most people would find strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange or hard to believe. I can see that. But even knowing that, I still can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the messages you think are being sent about you as being unusual or not something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say it’s unusual, yes. I mean, I know it probably sounds like something that wouldn’t normally happen. But it still feels real to me, like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you're misinterpreting these messages and they're not actually about you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I know there’s a possibility I’m misreading it. I do think about that. But even when I tell myself it might not be about me, I still can’t really shake the feeling that certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages in what they say or in the bulletin.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings that people are watching you and sending you messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been about eleven months now. The voices and the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight… that’s all been going on for around that long.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices earlier, can you tell me more about what you mean by voices?\n\nPatient: Sometimes when the house is quiet, I’ll hear my name called, or just a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or the porch. It’s clear enough that I’ll answer back before I remember nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they telling you to do something or are they more like they're talking about you?\n\nPatient: It’s more just my name, or a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice coming from the kitchen or the porch. I don’t really hear it giving me orders, it’s just clear enough that sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you think they're actually coming from outside your head or are they just your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s coming from outside my head, like from the kitchen or out on the porch. It doesn’t feel like just my own thoughts, because it sounds like my friend’s voice, and sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things - the messages, the voices, and the feeling of being watched - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes... I think they might be connected. Even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. And then I’ll see little messages in the bulletin, or hear something in what they say, and it feels like it’s about me. The voices feel like part of that sometimes, too.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone, do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express what you're trying to say?\n\nPatient: Yes, sometimes. I’ll start trying to explain one thing, like something about the mail or something at church, and then I kind of drift into talking about quilt patterns, or birds, or warnings I noticed. Then I can see the other person looking confused, and I realize I haven’t really said what I meant to say.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and feelings of being watched been constant over the past eleven months, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: They’ve pretty much been going on for about eleven months now. They’re not every single minute, but the voices, the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening through that whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful events or changes in your life around the time these feelings and voices started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes... it started not too long after my close friend died. That was about a year and five months ago. Things seemed to change after that.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the feeling of being watched ever stopped completely, even for a short period, since they started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think they’ve stopped completely. They come and go some during the day, but since it started, the voices and messages have been there off and on the whole eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where these voices and feelings of being watched stopped, and then came back?\n\nPatient: No, not really. They’ve eased up at times, but I don’t think they ever fully stopped and then came back. The voices and messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight, have been going on about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stick to one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I’ll start trying to explain something, like about the mail or something at church, and then somehow I’m talking about quilt patterns, or birds outside, or warnings I think I’m seeing. Then I notice the other person looks confused, like they don’t know what I’m trying to say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I get this feeling they’re watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say, but I can’t really say it’s some larger group behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the idea that people are watching you and sending you messages is something that most people would find strange or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange or hard to believe. I can see that. But even knowing that, I still can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the messages you think are being sent about you as being unusual or not something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say it’s unusual, yes. I mean, I know it probably sounds like something that wouldn’t normally happen. But it still feels real to me, like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you're misinterpreting these messages and they're not actually about you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I know there’s a possibility I’m misreading it. I do think about that. But even when I tell myself it might not be about me, I still can’t really shake the feeling that certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages in what they say or in the bulletin.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings that people are watching you and sending you messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been about eleven months now. The voices and the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight… that’s all been going on for around that long.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices earlier, can you tell me more about what you mean by voices?\n\nPatient: Sometimes when the house is quiet, I’ll hear my name called, or just a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or the porch. It’s clear enough that I’ll answer back before I remember nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they telling you to do something or are they more like they're talking about you?\n\nPatient: It’s more just my name, or a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice coming from the kitchen or the porch. I don’t really hear it giving me orders, it’s just clear enough that sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you think they're actually coming from outside your head or are they just your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s coming from outside my head, like from the kitchen or out on the porch. It doesn’t feel like just my own thoughts, because it sounds like my friend’s voice, and sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things - the messages, the voices, and the feeling of being watched - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes... I think they might be connected. Even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. And then I’ll see little messages in the bulletin, or hear something in what they say, and it feels like it’s about me. The voices feel like part of that sometimes, too.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone, do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express what you're trying to say?\n\nPatient: Yes, sometimes. I’ll start trying to explain one thing, like something about the mail or something at church, and then I kind of drift into talking about quilt patterns, or birds, or warnings I noticed. Then I can see the other person looking confused, and I realize I haven’t really said what I meant to say.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and feelings of being watched been constant over the past eleven months, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: They’ve pretty much been going on for about eleven months now. They’re not every single minute, but the voices, the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening through that whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful events or changes in your life around the time these feelings and voices started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes... it started not too long after my close friend died. That was about a year and five months ago. Things seemed to change after that.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the feeling of being watched ever stopped completely, even for a short period, since they started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think they’ve stopped completely. They come and go some during the day, but since it started, the voices and messages have been there off and on the whole eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where these voices and feelings of being watched stopped, and then came back?\n\nPatient: No, not really. They’ve eased up at times, but I don’t think they ever fully stopped and then came back. The voices and messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight, have been going on about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stick to one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I’ll start trying to explain something, like about the mail or something at church, and then somehow I’m talking about quilt patterns, or birds outside, or warnings I think I’m seeing. Then I notice the other person looks confused, like they don’t know what I’m trying to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt completely normal and none of these voices or feelings of being watched were present, before they started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Before all this started, yes, I think I felt normal. But for about eleven months now, the voices and messages and having trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been going on.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

The voices and the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight… that’s all been going on for around that long.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices earlier, can you tell me more about what you mean by voices?\n\nPatient: Sometimes when the house is quiet, I’ll hear my name called, or just a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or the porch. It’s clear enough that I’ll answer back before I remember nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they telling you to do something or are they more like they're talking about you?\n\nPatient: It’s more just my name, or a few words. It sounds like my friend’s voice coming from the kitchen or the porch. I don’t really hear it giving me orders, it’s just clear enough that sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you think they're actually coming from outside your head or are they just your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s coming from outside my head, like from the kitchen or out on the porch. It doesn’t feel like just my own thoughts, because it sounds like my friend’s voice, and sometimes I answer before I realize nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things - the messages, the voices, and the feeling of being watched - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes... I think they might be connected. Even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake this feeling that some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. And then I’ll see little messages in the bulletin, or hear something in what they say, and it feels like it’s about me. The voices feel like part of that sometimes, too.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone, do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express what you're trying to say?\n\nPatient: Yes, sometimes. I’ll start trying to explain one thing, like something about the mail or something at church, and then I kind of drift into talking about quilt patterns, or birds, or warnings I noticed. Then I can see the other person looking confused, and I realize I haven’t really said what I meant to say.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and feelings of being watched been constant over the past eleven months, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: They’ve pretty much been going on for about eleven months now. They’re not every single minute, but the voices, the messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening through that whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful events or changes in your life around the time these feelings and voices started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes... it started not too long after my close friend died. That was about a year and five months ago. Things seemed to change after that.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the feeling of being watched ever stopped completely, even for a short period, since they started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think they’ve stopped completely. They come and go some during the day, but since it started, the voices and messages have been there off and on the whole eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where these voices and feelings of being watched stopped, and then came back?\n\nPatient: No, not really. They’ve eased up at times, but I don’t think they ever fully stopped and then came back. The voices and messages, and the trouble keeping my thoughts straight, have been going on about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stick to one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I’ll start trying to explain something, like about the mail or something at church, and then somehow I’m talking about quilt patterns, or birds outside, or warnings I think I’m seeing. Then I notice the other person looks confused, like they don’t know what I’m trying to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt completely normal and none of these voices or feelings of being watched were present, before they started eleven months ago?\n\nPatient: Before all this started, yes, I think I felt normal. But for about eleven months now, the voices and messages and having trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of at least two weeks where you didn't have any of these voices, feelings of being watched, or trouble keeping your thoughts straight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had a stretch like that. They may get quieter sometimes, but the voices and messages and my thoughts getting mixed up have been going on for about eleven months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
